   - The common edges in all paths are (B, G) and (G, H).

1. **Identify the black nodes:** The two black nodes are E and N.
2. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - E's immediate neighboring nodes are S, B, and F.
   - N's immediate neighboring nodes are A, C, O, and G.

3. **Find all paths between E and N:**
   - E connects to B, which connects to J, H, I, and C.
   - N connects to C, which connects to B, H, and G.
   - Therefore, the paths are:
     - E → B → C → N
     - E → B → H → C → N
     - E → B → J → H → C → N
     - E → B → H → I → P → Q → J → H → C → N
     - E → B → J → H → I → P → Q → J → H → C → N
     - E → B → J → H → C → N
     - E → B → C → G → O → N

4. **Evaluate the paths:**
   - The paths all intersect at the node B. Therefore, removing the edge between E and B will disconnect E from B, and thus from N. However, since N can still connect via C and G, we need to remove edges involving C and G as well.

5. **Find the minimal cut set:**
   - Removing the edges (E, B), (N, C), and (N, G) will completely disconnect the two black nodes.

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3.

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(E, B), (N, C), (N, G)].

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (R and J) in the provided graph, we will proceed step-by-step as described:

1. **Examine the two black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are R and J.

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - The immediate neighboring nodes of R are C, B, G, and O.
   - The immediate neighboring nodes of J are I, G, and Q.

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - Node C connects to B, N, O, and G.
   - Node B connects to T, F, S, E, A, and Q.
   - Node G connects to C, B, H, I, O, and Q.
   - Node O connects to B, C, and Q.
   - Node I connects to Q, H, and J.
   - Node Q connects to B, I, and P.

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Path from R to J via R → G → I → J.
   - Path from R to J via R → C → B → Q → I → J.
   - Path from R to J via R → G → B → Q → I → J.
   - Path from R to J via R → C → O → Q → I → J.

   To disconnect R and J, we need to remove edges such that no path remains between them.

5. **Check for bottlenecks and minimum edge set:**
   - Removing the edge (R, G) does not suffice as R can still reach J via R → C → B → Q → I → J.
   - Removing the edge (J, I) does not suffice as R can still reach J via R → G → B → Q → I → J.
   - Removing both (R, G) and (R, C) will disconnect R from the rest of the graph containing J.
   - Similarly, removing (J, I) and (J, G) will disconnect J from the rest of the graph containing R.

   However, a more minimal cut would be to remove the edges that provide the shortest path between R and J:

   - Removing (R, G) and (J, G) ensures there are no remaining paths:
     - Removing (R, G) removes one direct connection from R to J via G.
     - Removing (J, G) ensures J is no longer reachable through G-based paths.
     - All remaining paths must contain B or C, both of which have alternative paths not intersecting R and J directly.

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 2:
**Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(R, G), (J, G)]**
